10.4 Dashboard JSON tab

editview=json opens the JSON of the whole draft document.

  • The text starts as the canonical text of the draft (6.15). When it has none, it is the JSON with an indent of two.
  • Each JSON text area has spellcheck="false", as the SQL text area has (10.3). The browser draws no wavy line in it.
  • The page checks the text 1 s after the last change, and when the user selects "Check":
    1. In the page: the JSON syntax and duplicate members, with their line and column.
    2. On the server: PUT api/dashboards/<uid>?validate_only=true, or POST api/dashboards?validate_only=true for a dashboard that is not saved yet.
  • The body of this request is the text {"document": and the text of the tab and }. Thus the server reads the exact text, with its duplicate members (6.2).
  • The page lists every error with its JSON pointer, its code and its message (4.37), up to MAX_ERRORS (4.35). A click on an error puts the cursor on the member when the page finds it.
  • "Apply" is active when the last check is valid. It replaces the draft with the canonical text of the check. "Format" replaces the text of the tab with this canonical text.
  • Text that is not applied stays in the draft as json_text (10.8). To leave the tab with such text, the user confirms in a dialog.

10.5 Layout edits

  • The fields of "Position" (10.3) change the grid numbers of a panel. Then the page normalizes the layout of the section.
  • "Move up" and "Move down" use the order of the panels of the section: by y, then x, then id. The panel changes its place with the previous or the next panel: the two panels exchange their x and y. Then the page normalizes the layout. The control is not active for the first or the last panel of the section.
  • To move a panel to another section, the user changes the JSON of the dashboard (10.4).

Normalization (FE-16) is the vertical compaction of the grid of Grafana. The page takes the panels of one section by y, then x, then id. For each panel it does these steps:

  1. The page starts with the current y of the panel.
  2. While y > 0 and the panel at y - 1 overlaps no panel that is already placed, y becomes y - 1.
  3. While the panel overlaps a placed panel P, y becomes P.y + P.h.
  4. The panel is now placed. x, w and h do not change.

The page normalizes after an add, a duplicate, a delete, a move and a change of "Position". It never normalizes the text of a JSON tab.

10.5a Drag and resize on the grid

  • In edit mode, the user drags the title bar of a panel to move it. The user drags the bottom-right corner of a panel to change its size.
  • A narrow screen (768 px or less) has no drag, because the grid is a flow (9.8). The form "Position" and the items "Move up" and "Move down" stay in all cases (9.26).
  • When the pointer moves less than 4 px, the action is a click, not a drag. A button, a link or the info icon in the title bar never starts a drag.
  • The dragged panel follows the pointer. A placeholder shows the cell of the drop. Its colour is the primary colour at 15 %.
  • The cell of a drag is x = round((left - 8) / P) and y = round((top - 8) / 38). left and top are the pixels from the corner of the grid. P is the width of one column plus 8 px.
  • The size of a resize is w = round((width + 8) / P) and h = round((height + 8) / 38).
  • The page keeps the cell in the section: 0 <= x <= 24 - w, y >= 0, 1 <= w <= 24 - x and 1 <= h <= 200.
  • Each new cell changes the layout of the section. The panels that the moved panel meets go above it when there is room (the two swap). Else they go below it. Then the compaction of 10.5 pulls each panel up.
  • The layout of a step is the input of the next step, as in Grafana. The same final cell can give two layouts after two different paths.
  • Escape or a cancelled pointer ends the drag. The draft does not change.
  • At the drop, the page changes the grid members of the panels in the draft, as the form "Position" does (10.5). The draft, the save and the conflict rules do not change.
  • Keys: when the focus is in a panel, Alt with an arrow key moves the panel by one cell. Alt with Shift and an arrow key changes the size by one unit. A status line gives the new cell. When the move changes nothing, the status line says "The panel cannot go there.".
  • A drag does not move a panel to another section. The user changes the JSON for that (10.5).
  • The draft grid draws the copies of each repeat (10.6). Only the first copy of a panel has the title bar handle, the resize corner and the menu button. A copy n from 2 is a view: it has none of them, and the keys of this section do not act on it.
  • A drag of the first copy of a panel with direction h changes y only. The page keeps x = 0 and w = 24. A resize of it changes h only. A drag or a resize of a panel with direction v changes x, y, w and h as for a panel, and the copies follow. The moves of 9.8c apply to the draft grid, so the panels below the copies are drawn at their displayed place. A drop writes the stored grid of the panels, never the displayed place.

10.6 Add, duplicate and delete a panel; sections

  • "Add panel" adds a panel to the last section, and "Add panel here" adds it to that section. The new panel has:
    • id: the highest panel id of the document plus 1;
    • type: table, with the "New" options of 9.19, title: "New panel" and description: the empty text;
    • grid: x = 0, y = the bottom of the section, w = 12, h = 8;
    • data.query: the first source of GET api/sources in id order, format = "table", sql = ["SELECT 1"], min_interval = null and max_data_points = null (4.15);
    • fields: defaults with the default thresholds of 9.14 only, and overrides = [];
    • transforms = [] and links = [].
  • When GET api/sources is empty, the page shows "No source is available to you." and adds nothing. After an add, the panel editor opens.
  • "Duplicate" copies the panel with the same title and a new id: the highest id plus 1. The copy has y = the original y plus its h. A reuse panel of the original keeps its source.
  • "Delete" opens the dialog "Delete the panel <title>?". When other panels reuse this panel, the page refuses and shows "The panels <titles> reuse this panel. Change them first.".
  • The dialog of "Delete" also lists the annotations that name the panel in panels (10.3b). The delete removes the id from each one in the same draft change. "Duplicate" gives the copy a new id that no panels list names.
  • "Add section" adds a section at the end, with the title "New section", collapsed = false and no panel.
  • "Rename" puts a text field in the section header. Enter applies and Escape cancels. An empty title shows an error.
  • The menu "Section actions" of a section header has the items "Move up", "Move down" and "Delete section". The first two items change the place of the section. The item "Delete section" opens the dialog "Delete the section <title>?". The section and its panels then leave the draft.
  • The page refuses "Delete section" for the last section, with the notice "A dashboard needs at least one section.". It also refuses it when a panel of another section reuses a panel of this section. The notice is "The panels <titles> reuse a panel of this section. Change them first.".

Text panel (4.33a):

  • "Add text panel" adds a panel to the last section. The item "Add text panel here" (section-<index>-add-text) of the menu "Section actions" adds it to that section. The new panel has these members:
    • id is the highest panel id of the document plus 1. type is text.
    • title is "New text". description is the empty text. transparent is false.
    • grid has x = 0, y = the bottom of the section, w = 12 and h = 8. links is [].
    • options has mode = markdown, and content = ["# Title", "", "Write text in Markdown."].
    • The panel has no data, no transforms and no fields.
  • The message "No source is available to you." of "Add panel" does not stop "Add text panel". After an add, the panel editor opens.
  • "Duplicate" copies the text with the same title and a new id, as for each panel. "Delete" opens the dialog of the panel. No panel reuses a text panel, so the dialog never lists one.

Placed controls (4.7a):

  • A person places a variable from its form (10.3b) or from the menu of "Add" (10.2). A person puts it back with "Put back in the top bar" in the menu of its frame. All of these change the draft only, as all edits do (10.2).
  • The draft grid draws the frame of each placed control. A drag, a resize and the keys of 10.5a change x, y, w and h of the placement, and nothing else. The rules of the grid (4.14) and the compaction of 10.5 apply to the placed controls and the panels together.
  • A move of a section ("Move up" or "Move down") changes the section of its placements. It does the same for the other section. "Delete section" removes the placements in the section. These variables are then back in the top bar. The dialog of the section says it when the section has a placement.
  • The entry "Edit the variable" of the menu of a frame opens the tab "Variables" of the settings with the form of that variable.

Repeat (4.13a):

  • The menu "Section actions" has the item "Repeat by a variable" (section-<index>-repeat). It opens a dialog (section-<index>-repeat-dialog) with a select of the variables of the draft that have the kind query or custom and multi = true (section-<index>-repeat-variable). The item "None" removes the member repeat. The buttons are "Apply" and "Cancel".
  • The item is not active in three cases, and its tooltip says why. The section has title = null, or a variable has a placement in the section, or the section holds a panel with repeat or with data.reuse.
  • A section with a repeat shows a badge in its header (section-<index>-repeat-badge) with the text "Repeats by <label>". The draft grid draws the copies. Only the first copy has the menu "Section actions". The copy n from 2 is a view.
  • A "Duplicate" of a panel that has a repeat keeps the repeat. The copy is below the original, so the page draws it below the region of the original (9.8c).
  • A "Delete" of a panel that has a repeat removes the stored panel, and so all its copies. A panel that has a repeat has no reuse panel (4.13a, rule 8), so the dialog never lists one for it.
  • "Move up" and "Move down" of a section keep its repeat. A section with a repeat has no placement, so no section index changes.
  • A control of a variable goes to the last section that has no repeat (4.7a, rule 11). The item "Place a variable in the grid" is not active when every section repeats.
  • The placed control of an open variable is the same control as in the bar (9.10). Its popup opens over the grid. A frame of 2 rows or more shows the field, and the combobox has the keys of 9.10. A move or a resize of the frame changes no value.
  • The list "Reuse a panel" of the panel editor offers no panel that repeats. It offers no panel of a section that repeats (4.13a, rule 8).

10.7 New dashboard, folders and move

These controls are on the list page (9.7). They exist only when editing is true, and when the access flags of 6.11 and 6.12 permit them (11.25).

  • "New dashboard" opens a dialog with "Title" (necessary) and "Folder" (a select of the folders with access.write).
    • The page makes a uid: 14 characters of [a-z0-9], from crypto.getRandomValues, with no bias.
    • It makes the smallest valid document of 4.5 with this uid and this title: description empty, tags = [], time = {from: "now-6h", to: "now", timezone: "browser", refresh: null}, cursor_sync = "off" and variables = []. It has one section {title: null, collapsed: false, panels: []}.
    • It sends POST api/dashboards with a new save_id (10.9). On 409 already_exists it makes a new uid and tries one more time.
    • It opens d/<uid>?edit=1.
  • "New folder" needs access.create. It is in the header of the list page (9.7). It opens a dialog with "Id" (the rule of 4.3 shows under the field) and "Title". It sends POST api/folders.
  • "Rename" and "Delete" of a folder are in the menu on the folder title (9.7).
  • "Rename" sends PUT api/folders/<id>. "Delete" asks for a confirmation and sends DELETE api/folders/<id>. 409 folder_not_empty gives "The folder holds dashboards. Move or delete them first.".
  • "Move" of a dashboard is in the menu of its card (9.7). It opens a select of the folders with access.write. It sends POST api/dashboards/<uid>/move with If-Match (the etag of the list item) and a new save_id.
  • A dialog shows the message of each error of 6.8 under its fields.
  • The buttons of a dialog come in this order: the safe choice first ("Cancel" or "Stay"), the action last.
  • A modal dialog is 500 px wide, and the import dialog is 520 px wide (10.12). Its top edge is at 10 % of the window height. The radius is 10 px, and the title is 18 px. On a phone the dialog has the full width. The delete and exit dialogs keep their texts, and one click confirms.

10.8 Draft in localStorage

The key is hune.draft.v1.<uid>. The value is:

json
{ "base_etag": "1b4f0e9870cf6b5e3a5b8c2d6e7f8a9b0c1d2e3f4a5b6c7d8e9f0a1b2c3d4e5f", "base_version": 12,
  "subject": "user:alice", "folder": "transit", "updated_at_ms": 1794232800000,
  "document": { "format_version": 3, "uid": "nodes" },
  "json_text": null, "save_id": null, "message": "" }
  • The page writes the draft 500 ms after the last change, and before the page unloads.
  • The page removes the draft after these events: a save that succeeds, "Revert" and "Discard", the choice "Load theirs" of 10.10, and a delete.
  • A dashboard with a draft shows a notice with "Resume" and "Discard". Its text is: "You have changes that are not saved, from <time>." "Resume" opens edit mode with the draft. The page shows this notice in the states view and panel-view. It shows it only to a user who can edit (10.2a, rule 8). "Resume" opens the state edit and closes the panel view. The page shows the same notice when the browser "Back" or "Forward" ends edit mode while the draft has changes (10.2a, rule 9).
  • When base_etag differs from the current etag, the notice adds: "The dashboard changed since then." The next save then gives the conflict screen (10.10).
  • The page removes, with a notice, a draft that is not valid JSON, and a draft whose document.uid differs from its key. It also removes a draft whose document.format_version is not the current one (4.42). The server refuses such a document. After the upgrade to format 3, the page removes a draft of format 2 with the notice "A draft of an older format was removed. Save or discard your drafts before an upgrade." So the people save or discard their drafts before the migration (4.42, item 5).
  • The member subject is the subject of the session (6.10) that wrote the draft. When it differs from the subject of the current session, the page removes the draft and does not show it. Thus a second person on a shared browser does not see the draft of the first person in the page. This removal loses the draft of the first person. Until a page of another subject removes it, the draft stays readable with the tools of the browser (12.18).
  • base_version is 0 for a uid with no version (6.12).
  • When localStorage fails (full, or a private window), the page shows "Hune cannot keep your draft in this browser. Save soon." The edit continues in memory.
  • While the draft differs from the base, the event beforeunload asks the question of the browser. A link of the page opens the dialog of 10.2 for "Exit". The browser "Back" and "Forward" do not open it: they keep the draft and show the notice above.
  • In edit mode, 409 dashboard_changed does not read the document again. The page shows "The dashboard changed. Save to compare your changes with the new version.".
  • Two tabs on one dashboard share one draft: the last write wins. The storage event tells the other tab, which shows "This dashboard is open for edit in another tab.".

10.9 Save

  1. The user selects "Save" (data-testid="save-button") or presses Ctrl+S. A drawer opens (the look is below). The tab "Details" shows the field "Message" (optional, 500 characters at most) and the button "Save".
  2. When the draft has no save_id, the page makes one and keeps it in the draft. The save_id is a UUID version 4 from crypto.getRandomValues. The high four bits of byte 6 are 0100, and the high two bits of byte 8 are 10. The text is lower case, in the canonical form.
  3. The page sends PUT api/dashboards/<uid> with If-Match: "<base_etag>", Idempotency-Key: <save_id> and the body {"message": "<message>", "document": <draft document>}. A dashboard that is not saved yet uses POST api/dashboards (10.7).
  4. The page acts on the answer with the table below.
  5. A change of the draft after a save that failed removes save_id. Thus the next save is a new attempt.
AnswerThe page
200It removes the draft, takes the new etag and version as the base and ends edit mode (10.2a, rule 7). A panel editor that a panel view opened gives the panel view again, with the saved panel. It shows "Saved as version <n>.", or "Nothing changed." for noop: true, and each warning.
409 conflictIt opens the conflict screen (10.10).
422 invalid_documentIt lists the errors with their pointers. A click opens the JSON tab at the member. The draft stays.
422 idempotency_key_reusedIt makes a new save_id and shows "Select Save again.".
503 storage_unavailable, a network error, or no answer after 30 sIt shows "Saving is not possible now. Your draft is kept in this browser." with "Retry". "Retry" sends the same request with the same save_id.
403 forbidden, 403 editing_disabledIt shows the message. The draft stays.
401 unauthenticatedThe full page of 9.6.
413 payload_too_largeIt shows "The dashboard is too large.".
507 storage_fullIt shows "The store is full. Tell the operator.".
Another errorIt shows the code and the message. The draft stays.

The drawer of the save dialog (data-testid="save-dialog") has this look:

  • It is at the right edge of the window, with the full height. Its width is 50 vw. Up to 768 px it is the window less 16 px.
  • It has a title, a muted subtitle with the title of the dashboard, and a close button.
  • It has two tabs, "Details" and "Changes". The arrow keys move between the tabs.
  • "Details" has the field "Message" with its count of characters (500 at most), the buttons "Cancel" and "Save", and the table of errors. The buttons hide on "Changes".
  • "Changes" shows the line diff from the stored version to the draft (10.15). The tab shows the number of changed lines. When the diff fails, the tab shows "The changes are not available now.".
  • The test ids and the answers of the table above do not change.

The same save_id on "Retry" makes the save safe after a lost answer (6.6). For example, during a PostgreSQL failover, the first request can commit and lose its answer. The retry then gets the stored answer, and the store holds one version.

10.10 Conflict screen

409 conflict on a save opens the conflict screen (data-testid="conflict-screen"). It shows:

  • The version of the current document, saved_by and saved_at (in the zone of the dashboard), from the error members (6.7). Above them is the text "The dashboard changed after you opened it."
  • A line diff (10.15) from theirs to mine. "Theirs" is the canonical text of GET api/dashboards/<uid>?download=true. "Mine" is the canonical text of the draft from validate_only=true.
  • When the draft is not valid, "mine" is its JSON with an indent of two spaces. The screen then adds the text "Your draft is not a valid document."

Three choices:

  1. "Load theirs and keep my draft". The page downloads the draft as <uid>-draft-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json, and shows it in a read-only side panel "My draft" with "Copy". Then it removes the draft and loads theirs as the new base. Edit mode continues on theirs, and the user copies the changes by hand. The side panel stays until the page closes.
  2. "Overwrite with mine". A dialog says "Their version stays in the history." After the confirmation, the page sends PUT with If-Match = current_etag, a new save_id and the draft. The answer follows 10.9. A new 409 opens the conflict screen again, with the newer version.
  3. "Cancel". The screen closes. Edit mode continues with the draft and the old base.

The focus goes to the first choice when the screen opens. Escape is "Cancel".

Two other cases of the same 409:

  • A move. When current_etag equals the base etag and current_folder differs from the folder of the page, the conflict comes from a move (5.5, rule 2). The screen shows no diff, and offers "Save in the new folder" and "Cancel". Its text is: "The dashboard moved to the folder <title> after you opened it."
  • "Save in the new folder" sends the same PUT with a new save_id. The server updated its catalog after the conflict (6.15), so the save can pass.
  • A delete. When GET api/dashboards/<uid>?download=true gives 404 for "theirs", the dashboard was deleted after the conflict. The screen shows "The dashboard was deleted after you opened it." It offers "Download my draft" and "Cancel". An operator can bring the dashboard back with hune restore (10.13).

10.11 History: list, view, diff and restore

"History" in the menu "More actions" (9.9) opens editview=history. Each subject that can read the dashboard can read its history (dashboard.read, 11).

List (data-testid="history-list"):

  • The columns are "Version", "Kind", "Saved at" (in the zone of the dashboard), "Saved by", "Message" and "Size". The newest version is first, with the mark "current". Under 900 px the columns "Kind", "Saved by" and "Size" hide.
  • "Load more" reads the next page while next is not null (6.24).
  • Each row has "View" and "Compare with current".
  • A row that is not current, not a deletion version and not an external version without bytes also has "Restore". This needs editing = true and access.write on the dashboard.
  • The user can select two rows with their check boxes. "Compare" then shows the diff from the older to the newer one.

View (editview=history&version=<n>):

  • The page shows version n as a read-only dashboard, with the banner "Version <n> of <time>, by <subject>. This view is read-only.".
  • The banner has "Restore this version" (when permitted), "Compare with current", "Export this version" and "Back to the history".
  • Each panel shows the text "Data not shown".
  • The banner has one button "Show data" for the whole page, when the user can use the preview. This is true when editing is true and each source of the version is in GET api/sources.
  • "Show data" runs each panel of the version that has a query with the preview route (6.22), three at a time. It uses the variables of the version. The options of its query variables come from the preview with variable.
  • A version that the current rules cannot read (422 stored_document_invalid) shows its errors. It also shows its stored text from download=true (6.17, 4.42). "Restore" is not active for it.
  • An external version without bytes (6.17) has no document to show. The view shows the text "The file of this version was not a valid document. Hune keeps only its etag: <etag>." "Restore", "Compare" and "Export" are not active for it. The history list shows this version with the size "-".

Diff (editview=history&base=<a>&version=<b>, data-testid="diff-view"):

  • a and b are version numbers, or current. "Compare with current" opens base=<n>&version=current.
  • The page reads both versions with download=true (6.14, 6.17). It shows the line diff of 10.15 with the title "Changes from version <a> to version <b>".

Restore:

  • "Restore" opens the dialog "Restore version <n> as a new version?" with the field "Message".
  • The page sends POST api/dashboards/<uid>/restore with If-Match = the current etag and a new save_id.
  • 200 gives "Version <n> is restored as version <m>." and the page shows the current dashboard again.
  • 409 conflict gives "The dashboard changed. Load the history again.".

10.12 Import and export of one document

Export:

  • "Export" of the dashboard menu, of the menu of a card and of a version view reads download=true (6.14, 6.17).
  • The browser saves the canonical bytes as <uid>.json, or <uid>-v<n>.json for a version.

Import (the list page, when editing is true and one folder has access.write):

  1. The user selects a .json file. A file larger than limits.document_bytes (6.10) gives an error. The page reads the file as UTF-8.
  2. A Grafana file stops the import, with the text "This is a Grafana file. Use the command hune import-grafana." A Grafana file is JSON with one of these:
    • an apiVersion that starts with dashboard.grafana.app/;
    • the members schemaVersion and panels;
    • a member dashboard that holds panels.
  3. A dialog of 520 px shows the uid of the document and a select of the folders with access.write. The dialog has a drop zone for the file. The zone has the title "Upload dashboard JSON file" and the hint "Drag and drop here or click to browse". It also has a button "Choose file" and the name of the chosen file. A file that the user drops on the zone counts as a chosen file.
  4. The page sends POST api/dashboards?validate_only=true with the exact text, as 10.4 does. Each error shows with its pointer, and the import stops until the user gives a valid file.
  5. The page reads GET api/dashboards/<uid>. When it gives 200, the uid exists. The dialog then offers two choices. "Replace the current dashboard" shows the line diff from the current document to the file. After a confirmation, it sends PUT with If-Match = the current etag and a new save_id. The folder stays the same.
    • 422 stored_document_invalid also means that the uid exists. The page then takes the etag of its list item (6.12), and shows the stored bytes of download=true in place of the current document. It offers the same two choices.
  6. "Import as a new dashboard" makes a new uid (10.7) and puts it in the member uid. The page checks the document again with validate_only=true, then sends POST api/dashboards.
  7. When the uid does not exist, the page sends POST api/dashboards with the folder of the dialog. A 403 of step 5 (mode authzen) also leads to this step. There, 409 already_exists gives the text "A dashboard with this uid exists, but you cannot read it."
  8. After a success, the page opens the dashboard.

The store keeps the canonical bytes of the imported document (4.38), not the bytes of the file.

10.13 Delete and restore of a dashboard

  • "Delete" exists in the menu "More actions" of the dashboard page (9.9) and as the bin of a card in the list page (9.7). It needs editing true and access.write. It opens the dialog "Delete the dashboard <title>? It leaves the list. An operator can bring it back." with "Delete" and "Cancel".
  • The page sends DELETE api/dashboards/<uid> with If-Match = the etag that it holds and a new save_id (6.16). The store keeps a deletion version and the history (5.4).
  • 200 opens the list page with the notice "The dashboard <title> is deleted.". The page removes the draft of the uid.
  • After a lost answer, the page sends the delete again with the same save_id. A 404 not_found on this retry counts as a success, because the first delete was done (6.6).
  • 409 conflict gives "The dashboard changed. Read it again and try again.".
  • In v1, the subcommand restore of the server brings a deleted dashboard back (5.40). The page has no list of deleted dashboards in v1 (10.16).

10.14 States that stop edits

StateThe page
editing = false in the session (no store is the primary store)No "Edit", "New dashboard", "New folder", "Import", "Move", "Delete", "Restore" or folder control. The history list, the views and the diffs stay.
read_only = true on a dashboard or a folder (the store is read-only, 5.42)A lock on the card and in the header of the editor. The editor opens read-only. The page offers "New dashboard", "New folder" and "Move" only in the primary store. A write that the page sends gets 403 read_only_store, and the page shows its message.
stale = "file_invalid" on a dashboard (5.44)A warning on the card and in the header of the dashboard: the page shows the last valid state, and the file of the volume is not valid now. The text never holds a path or a message of the parser.
access.write = false for a dashboard or a folder, or access.create = false (mode authzen, 11.25)The same controls are absent for this dashboard or this folder. "New folder" is absent without access.create.
A source of the dashboard is not in GET api/sourcesEdit mode opens, with the notice "A save needs the query right on the source <id>." (6.9). "Run" is absent for this source.
503 storage_unavailable on a writeThe notice of 10.9. Edit mode stays.
503 pdp_unavailableThe notice of 9.6. The controls stay. The server refuses the request (fail closed).
A URL with edit, editPanel or editview=json or settings, and the user cannot edit (a deep link, "Back", "Forward" or a reload)The page shows the state of 10.2a, rule 1, with no edit names. It shows the notice "You cannot edit this dashboard.". The draft stays in the browser. The page shows no "Resume".
The user opens editview=history and cannot editThe history shows (10.11). It has no "Restore".

The server checks each operation. The absence of a control is only a display rule (11).

10.15 Line diff

  • The vector id of the diff is FE-17.
  • The input is two texts. The page splits each text at each LF into lines.
  • The algorithm is the shortest edit script of Myers (O(ND)), on exact line equality.
  • The output has hunks with 3 lines of context. Each hunk starts with @@ -a,b +c,d @@.
  • Each line starts with (no change), - (removed) or + (added), as text. Two gutter columns give the line numbers of both sides. A removed line and an added line also have their colour tokens.
  • The diff has a limit: an edit distance of 10,000 lines, and 100,000 lines in a text. Past it, the page offers the download of each text, with this text: "The two versions differ too much to show the changes."
  • The diff module is a pure function, and the tests run it with Node (16).

Example: from ["a", "b", "c"] to ["a", "x", "c"], the diff is one hunk @@ -1,3 +1,3 @@ with the lines a, -b, +x and c.

10.16 What is in v1.1

These items are not in v1. Each one needs a reviewed design before its work starts (18):

  • upload of a Grafana file in the page;
  • a list of deleted dashboards and their restore in the page;
  • a diff side by side;
  • completion of names in the SQL text.

Drag and resize of the panels on the grid (10.5a) and the forms of 10.3a and 10.3b are done. They are not in this list.
